### 1. **How `_synthesis_coverage` is called and what data it has access to**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`

**Function signature (lines 92-103):**
```python
def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review findings in synthesis."""
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    for c in clusters.values():
        all_cluster_ids.update(c.get("finding_ids", []))
    review_ids = [
        fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
        if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
    ]
    organized = sum(1 for fid in review_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    return organized, len(review_ids), clusters
```

**Key limitation:** `_synthesis_coverage` only pulls from `plan["queue_order"]`, which is a **restricted list** and does NOT contain all open review findings.

**All callers of `_synthesis_coverage` (lines 92, 213, 219, 795, 831, 885, 1291, 1526):**
1. Line 213: `_print_progress()` — prints cluster state and unclustered findings
2. Line 219: `_apply_completion()` — COMPLETION FLOW **HAS ACCESS TO `state`** (line 226)
3. Line 795: `_cmd_synthesize_complete()` — fold-confirm with attestation
4. Line 831: `_cmd_synthesize_complete()` — coverage verification before requiring strategy
5. Line 885: `_cmd_synthesize_complete()` — final coverage summary
6. Line 1291: Part of reflection handler — coverage display
7. Line 1526: Part of completion summary display

**Critical: `_apply_completion` HAS STATE ACCESS:**
```python
def _apply_completion(args: argparse.Namespace, plan: dict, strategy: str) -> None:
    """Shared completion logic: update meta, remove synthesis::pending, save."""
    organized, total, clusters = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
    
    # ... (lines 225-226)

    state = runtime.state
    current_hash = review_finding_snapshot_hash(state)
    
    # Gets ALL open review findings from state (lines 231-234):

        fid for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items()
        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
    )
    meta["synthesized_ids"] = open_review_ids
```

**Status of `_print_progress` (lines 168-214):**

- **Receives `open_findings` as a parameter** (not from state)

### 2. **The structure of review finding IDs**

**Finding ID format examples from tests (line grep `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/review/test_work_queue.py`):**
```
review::.::holistic::initialization_coupling::abc12345
review::.::holistic::mid_level_elegance::split::abc12345
review::.::holistic::naming_quality::abc12345
review::.::holistic_unreviewed
review::src/a.py::changed
review::src/a.py::legacy
review::src/a.py::logic
review::src/a.py::naming
```

**ID construction from `make_finding()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` (lines 142-170):**
```python
def make_finding(
    detector: str,
    file: str,
    name: str,
    *,
    tier: int,
    confidence: str,
    summary: str,
    detail: dict | None = None,
) -> Finding:
    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
    rfile = rel(file)
    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
    # ...
```

**ID parsing rules from `_matches_pattern()` (lines 176-199):**
- Full ID match (exact)
- Glob pattern match with `*`
- Prefix match (ends with `::`)
- **Bare 8-char hex suffix match** (e.g., `abc12345` matches any ID ending with `::abc12345`)
- Detector name match
- File path match
- **Name segment fallback** — last `::` segment can be matched as bare name

**Display in `short_finding_id()` from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/display.py` (lines 6-16):**
```python
def short_finding_id(fid: str) -> str:
    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display."""
    if "::" in fid:
        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
        if len(suffix) >= 8:
            return suffix[:8]
    return fid
```

**Structure summary:**
- **Standard format:** `detector::file_path::descriptive_name::hash_suffix` (variable number of `::` segments)
- **Holistic review format:** `review::.::holistic::dimension::specific_aspect::8char_hex` (6 segments)
- **Per-file review format:** `review::file/path.py::descriptor` (3 segments)
- **Second-to-last segment is NOT always the descriptive name** — the structure varies by detector type
- The **last segment before the final hash** in holistic reviews contains dimension names
- Hashes are **8-character hex suffixes** appended after final `::`

### 3. **How `_apply_completion` handles coverage check and state access**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`, lines 217-259

**State access flow:**
1. Line 225: `runtime = command_runtime(args)` — loads runtime from args
2. Line 226: `state = runtime.state` — **FULL STATE DICT IS AVAILABLE**
3. Lines 231-235: Computes ALL open review/concerns finding IDs from state:
```python
open_review_ids = sorted(
    fid for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items()
    if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
)
meta["synthesized_ids"] = open_review_ids  # Records all open review IDs
```

**Coverage check flow (in caller `_cmd_synthesize_complete`, lines 831-843):**
```python
organized, total, clusters = _synthesis_coverage(plan)  # Uses only queue_order

if total > 0 and organized == 0:
    print(colorize("  Cannot complete: no findings have been organized into clusters.", "red"))
    print(colorize(f"  {total} findings are waiting.", "dim"))
    return

if total > 0 and organized < total:
    remaining = total - organized
    print(colorize(
        f"  Warning: {remaining}/{total} findings are not yet in any cluster.",
        "yellow",
    ))
```

**Problem:** The check uses `total` from `_synthesis_coverage(plan)` which is **only queue_order review IDs**, NOT all open review findings in state. This means:
- A finding in state but not in queue_order is invisible to this check
- The warning message shows incomplete coverage metrics

### 4. **What `queue_order` contains vs open review findings in state**

**Queue order is built from state at multiple points:**

**Initial population (from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py`, lines 28-80):**
- `build_finding_items()` iterates `state.get("findings", {})` 
- Filters for open, scoped, non-suppressed findings
- Creates items dict for ALL detectors (review, concerns, mechanical)
- **These items are NOT directly added to queue_order** — they're used by the work-queue builder

**Queue order gets populated from reconciliation (from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile.py`, lines 230-268):**
```python
def sync_plan_after_review_import(
    plan: PlanModel,
    state: StateModel,
) -> ReviewImportSyncResult | None:
    """Sync plan queue after review import."""
    new_ids = compute_new_finding_ids(plan, state)  # New review/concerns IDs
    if not new_ids:
        return None
    
    # Add new finding IDs to end of queue_order so they have position
    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    existing = set(order)
    added: list[str] = []
    for fid in sorted(new_ids):
        if fid not in existing:
            order.append(fid)  # ONLY NEW IDs are added
            added.append(fid)
```

**What `compute_new_finding_ids()` returns (from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/stale_dimensions.py`, lines 270-281):**
```python
def compute_new_finding_ids(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
    """Return the set of open review/concerns finding IDs added since last synthesis."""
    meta = plan.get("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
    synthesized = set(meta.get("synthesized_ids", []))
    current = {
        fid for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items()
        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
    }
    return current - synthesized if synthesized else set()  # Only returns DELTA, not all
```

**Key difference:**
- **`queue_order`** = manually promoted/positioned items + items added during synthesis workflow + new findings after last synthesis completion
- **Open findings in state** = ALL open review/concerns findings from the last scan/import
- **Missing from queue_order**: previously synthesized findings that haven't been re-surfaced (those are in `meta["synthesized_ids"]`)

**Example scenario:**
1. Scan finds 10 review findings, first synthesis completes, records all 10 in `synthesized_ids`
2. Review import adds 5 new review findings
3. `queue_order` now has only the 5 new findings (not the original 10)
4. State has 15 open review findings total
5. `_synthesis_coverage(plan)` counts only 5, missing 10 previously synthesized ones

---

### Summary Table

| Area | Finding |
|------|---------|
| **_synthesis_coverage data access** | Only `plan["queue_order"]` — subset of all open review findings |
| **_apply_completion state access** | HAS FULL STATE via `command_runtime(args).state` (line 226) |
| **_print_progress data source** | Receives `open_findings` dict as parameter, not limited to queue_order |
| **Review ID format** | Variable length, `detector::file::...::8char_hex`, last segment before hash is context-dependent |
| **ID parsing** | Supports full match, glob, prefix, bare hex suffix, detector, file path, name segment |
| **Coverage gap** | queue_order only contains promoted + newly added findings, not previously synthesized ones |
| **Completion checker call stack** | `_cmd_synthesize_complete()` → `_synthesis_coverage(plan)` → uses incomplete total from queue_order |

Design a plan to fix 5 bugs found by sub-agent testing of the desloppify synthesis workflow. Here's the context:

## Bug 1 (Critical): `_synthesis_coverage` uses `queue_order` instead of all open review findings

**File**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/synthesize_handlers.py`, line 92-103

Current code:
```python
def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    review_ids = [
        fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
        if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
    ]
```

Problem: `queue_order` has only 1 review finding, while state has 46. All 46 are in clusters but coverage shows "1/1".

**Callers** (7 total, all in synthesize_handlers.py):
- `_print_progress` (line 213) — already has `open_findings` dict as param
- `_apply_completion` (line 219) — has access to `state` via `command_runtime(args).state`
- `_cmd_synthesize_complete` (lines 795, 831, 885) — has `plan` and `args`
- Dashboard areas (lines 1291, 1526) — have `si.open_findings`

**Solution approach**: Change `_synthesis_coverage` to accept an `open_review_ids` parameter (set of IDs) instead of pulling from `queue_order`. The callers already have access to the right data:
- In `_apply_completion`, it already computes `open_review_ids` from state (line 231-234)
- In `_print_progress`, `open_findings` is passed in
- In `_cmd_synthesize_complete`, `command_runtime(args).state` is available
- In the dashboard, `si.open_findings` contains the review findings

This avoids adding a `state` dependency to the function signature.

## Bug 2 (Medium): Name-segment matching doesn't help for review findings

**File**: `desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`, lines 193-199

Current code adds a last-segment match. But holistic review finding IDs look like:
```
review::.::holistic::cross_module_architecture::facade_hub_coupling::7fd735cf
```
The last segment is always `7fd735cf` (8-char hex). The descriptive name `facade_hub_coupling` is the second-to-last segment.

ALL 46 current review findings are 6-segment with 8-char hex endings. The `_HEX8_RE` matcher already handles the last segment. The name-segment match is redundant for these but useful for non-hashed IDs.

**Solution**: Also try matching against the second-to-last segment when the last segment is an 8-char hex. So `facade_hub_coupling` matches `review::.::holistic::...::facade_hub_coupling::7fd735cf`.

Only when:
- Pattern has no `::`
- Finding ID has 3+ segments
- Last segment matches `_HEX8_RE` (so it's a hashed ID)
- Second-to-last segment exactly equals pattern

## Bug 3 (Minor): Cluster overlap warning fires on all existing members

**File**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py`, lines 73-87

Current code:
```python
member_set = set(plan["clusters"][cluster_name].get("finding_ids", []))
```
This checks ALL members (old + new) against other clusters. Should only check the newly-added `finding_ids`.

**Fix**: Replace `member_set` with `set(finding_ids)` (the IDs just added).

## Bug 4 (Minor): Step display double-numbering

**File**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py`, lines 263-265

```python
for i, step in enumerate(steps, 1):
    print(colorize(f"    {i}. {step}", "dim"))
```

When user passes `"1. Audit..."`, output shows `1. 1. Audit...`. Other display locations use bullets (`- step`).

**Fix**: Strip leading number+period from step text before displaying with auto-numbering, or switch to bullet display like other locations.

## Bug 5 (No code change needed): First `--complete` produces no output

The thorough agent reported this but it ran in a worktree with older code. This is likely a non-TTY stdout buffering issue in the worktree subprocess, not a product bug. Skip.

## Tests to add

- `test_synthesis_coverage.py`: Add test showing coverage uses provided review IDs, not queue_order
- `test_suffix_matching.py`: Add test for second-to-last segment matching (descriptive name)
- Existing overlap/step tests if they exist, otherwise note for manual verification

## Design constraints
- `_synthesis_coverage` callers must pass the review finding IDs. Don't add a `state` parameter — callers already have the data.
- Name-segment matching should be exact match only (not substring/prefix)
- Keep all changes minimal and focused

    41→def finding_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    42→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    43→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    44→        return True
    45→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"

    47→        file_path.startswith(prefix)

    66→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    67→            continue

    69→            continue

   107→    return None
   108→
   109→
   110→def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   111→    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   112→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   113→    matched_ids = [
   114→        finding_id
   115→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
   116→        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
   117→    ]
   118→    now = utc_now()
   119→    for finding_id in matched_ids:
   120→        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
   121→        finding["suppressed"] = True
   122→        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
   123→        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   124→    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
   125→    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
   126→
   127→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   128→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   129→    return len(matched_ids)
   130→
   131→
   132→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   133→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""
   134→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   135→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   136→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   137→    if pattern not in ignores:
   138→        ignores.append(pattern)
   139→    return remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)
   140→
   141→
   142→def make_finding(
   143→    detector: str,
   144→    file: str,
   145→    name: str,
   146→    *,
   147→    tier: int,
   148→    confidence: str,
   149→    summary: str,
   150→    detail: dict | None = None,
   151→) -> Finding:
   152→    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
   153→    rfile = rel(file)
   154→    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   155→    now = utc_now()
   156→    return {
   157→        "id": finding_id,
   158→        "detector": detector,
   159→        "file": rfile,
   160→        "tier": tier,
   161→        "confidence": confidence,
   162→        "summary": summary,
   163→        "detail": detail or {},
   164→        "status": "open",
   165→        "note": None,
   166→        "first_seen": now,
   167→        "last_seen": now,
   168→        "resolved_at": None,
   169→        "reopen_count": 0,
   170→    }
   171→
   172→
   173→_HEX8_RE = re.compile(r'^[0-9a-f]{8}$')
   174→
   175→
   176→def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
   177→    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, or path."""
   178→    if finding_id == pattern:
   179→        return True
   180→    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern):
   181→        return True
   182→    if "::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern):
   183→        return True
   184→    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and finding_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
   185→        return True
   186→    if (
   187→        finding.get("detector") == pattern
   188→        or finding["file"] == pattern
   189→        or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   190→    ):
   191→        return True
   192→
   193→    # Name-segment fallback: bare name matches the last ::segment of the ID
   194→    if "::" not in pattern and "::" in finding_id:
   195→        name_segment = finding_id.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
   196→        if name_segment == pattern:
   197→            return True
   198→
   199→    return False
   200→
```

    35→def _cmd_cluster_create(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    36→    name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
    37→    description: str | None = getattr(args, "description", None)

    44→        return
    45→    append_log_entry(
    46→        plan, "cluster_create", cluster_name=name, actor="user",
    47→        detail={"description": description, "action": action},
    48→    )
    49→    save_plan(plan)
    50→    print(colorize(f"  Created cluster: {name}", "green"))
    51→

    53→def _cmd_cluster_add(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    54→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    55→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    56→        return
    57→
    58→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
    59→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    60→
    61→    plan = load_plan()
    62→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    63→    if not finding_ids:
    64→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    65→        _print_pattern_hints()
    66→        return
    67→    try:
    68→        count = add_to_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)
    69→    except ValueError as ex:
    70→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
    71→        return
    72→
    73→    # Check for overlap with other manual clusters
    74→    member_set = set(plan["clusters"][cluster_name].get("finding_ids", []))
    75→    for other_name, other_cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    76→        if other_name == cluster_name or other_cluster.get("auto"):
    77→            continue
    78→        other_ids = set(other_cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    79→        if not other_ids:
    80→            continue
    81→        overlap = member_set & other_ids
    82→        if len(overlap) > len(other_ids) * 0.5:
    83→            print(colorize(
    84→                f"  Warning: {len(overlap)} finding(s) also in cluster '{other_name}' "
    85→                f"({len(overlap)}/{len(other_ids)} = {int(len(overlap)/len(other_ids)*100)}% overlap).",
    86→                "yellow",
    87→            ))
    88→
    89→    append_log_entry(
    90→        plan, "cluster_add", finding_ids=finding_ids, cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user",
    91→    )
    92→    save_plan(plan)
    93→    print(colorize(f"  Added {count} item(s) to cluster {cluster_name}.", "green"))
    94→

   217→def _apply_completion(args: argparse.Namespace, plan: dict, strategy: str) -> None:
   218→    """Shared completion logic: update meta, remove synthesis::pending, save."""
   219→    organized, total, clusters = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
   220→

   228→
   229→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   230→    meta["finding_snapshot_hash"] = current_hash
   231→    open_review_ids = sorted(
   232→        fid for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items()

   235→    meta["synthesized_ids"] = open_review_ids
   236→    if strategy.strip().lower() != "same":

   238→    meta["trigger"] = "manual_synthesis"
   239→    meta["last_completed_at"] = utc_now()
   240→    # Archive stages before clearing so previous analysis is preserved
   241→    stages = meta.get("synthesis_stages", {})
   242→    if stages:

   244→            "completed_at": utc_now(),

   246→            "strategy": strategy if strategy.strip().lower() != "same" else meta.get("strategy_summary", ""),
   247→        }
   248→    meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}  # clear stages on completion
   249→    meta.pop("stage_refresh_required", None)
   250→    meta.pop("stage_snapshot_hash", None)
   251→
   252→    save_plan(plan)
   253→
   254→    cluster_count = len([c for c in clusters.values() if c.get("finding_ids")])

   256→    effective_strategy = strategy if strategy.strip().lower() != "same" else meta.get("strategy_summary", "")
   257→    if effective_strategy:
   258→        print(colorize(f"  Strategy: {effective_strategy}", "cyan"))
   259→    print(colorize("  Run `desloppify next` to start implementation.", "green"))

   266→def _cmd_stage_observe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   267→    """Record the OBSERVE stage: agent analyses themes and root causes.
   268→
   269→    No citation gate — the point is genuine analysis, not ID-stuffing.
   270→    Just requires a 100-char report describing what the agent observed.
   271→    """

   273→
   274→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   275→    state = runtime.state
   276→    plan = load_plan()
   277→
   278→    # Auto-start: inject synthesis::pending if not present
   279→    if SYNTHESIS_ID not in plan.get("queue_order", []):
   280→        order = plan.setdefault("queue_order", [])
   281→        order.insert(0, SYNTHESIS_ID)
   282→        meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   283→        meta["synthesis_stages"] = {}
   284→        save_plan(plan)
   285→        print(colorize("  Synthesis auto-started (injected synthesis::pending).", "cyan"))
   286→
   287→    meta = plan.setdefault("epic_synthesis_meta", {})
   288→    stages = meta.setdefault("synthesis_stages", {})
   289→    existing_stage = stages.get("observe")
   290→
   291→    # Jump-back: reuse existing report if no --report provided
   292→    is_reuse = False
   293→    if not report and existing_stage and existing_stage.get("report"):
   294→        report = existing_stage["report"]
   295→        is_reuse = True
   296→    elif not report:
   297→        print(colorize("  --report is required for --stage observe.", "red"))
   298→        print(colorize("  Write an analysis of the findings: themes, root causes, contradictions.", "dim"))
   299→        print(colorize("  Identify findings that contradict each other (opposite recommendations).", "dim"))

1→"""Tests for _synthesis_coverage filtering to review findings only."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.app.commands.plan.synthesize_handlers import _synthesis_coverage
     6→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan
     7→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import SYNTHESIS_ID
     8→
     9→
    10→def _plan_with_queue(*finding_ids: str, clustered: list[str] | None = None) -> dict:
    11→    """Build a plan with given queue items and optional clustered IDs."""
    12→    plan = empty_plan()
    13→    plan["queue_order"] = [SYNTHESIS_ID] + list(finding_ids)
    14→    if clustered:
    15→        plan["clusters"]["test-cluster"] = {
    16→            "name": "test-cluster",
    17→            "finding_ids": list(clustered),
    18→            "description": "Test",
    19→            "action_steps": ["step 1"],
    20→            "auto": False,
    21→        }
    22→    return plan
    23→
    24→
    25→class TestSynthesisCoverage:
    26→    def test_coverage_excludes_non_review_items(self):
    27→        """Non-review queue items (mechanical findings) don't inflate total."""
    28→        plan = _plan_with_queue(
    29→            "review::test.py::naming_issue",
    30→            "review::test.py::coupling_issue",
    31→            "naming-convention::test.py",  # mechanical — should be excluded
    32→            "unused-import::test.py",       # mechanical — should be excluded
    33→            clustered=["review::test.py::naming_issue"],
    34→        )
    35→        organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
    36→        assert total == 2  # only review findings
    37→        assert organized == 1
    38→
    39→    def test_coverage_counts_review_findings_only(self):
    40→        """Review and concerns findings are correctly counted."""
    41→        plan = _plan_with_queue(
    42→            "review::a.py::issue1",
    43→            "concerns::b.py::issue2",
    44→            "review::c.py::issue3",
    45→            "unused-import::d.py",
    46→            clustered=["review::a.py::issue1", "concerns::b.py::issue2"],
    47→        )
    48→        organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
    49→        assert total == 3  # 2 review + 1 concerns
    50→        assert organized == 2
    51→
    52→    def test_coverage_empty_queue(self):
    53→        """Empty queue returns (0, 0, clusters)."""
    54→        plan = _plan_with_queue()  # only SYNTHESIS_ID
    55→        organized, total, clusters = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
    56→        assert organized == 0
    57→        assert total == 0
    58→        assert isinstance(clusters, dict)
    59→
    60→    def test_coverage_excludes_synthesis_id(self):
    61→        """The SYNTHESIS_ID sentinel is never counted."""
    62→        plan = _plan_with_queue("review::test.py::issue1")
    63→        _, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan)
    64→        assert total == 1  # SYNTHESIS_ID excluded
    65→

1→"""Tests for 8-char hex suffix matching in _matches_pattern."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import _matches_pattern
     6→
     7→
     8→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     9→# Helpers
    10→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11→
    12→_SAMPLE_ID = "review::.::holistic::dim::name::f41b3eb7"
    13→_SAMPLE_FINDING = {"detector": "review", "file": "test.py"}
    14→
    15→
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→# Suffix match tests
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→
    20→
    21→def test_suffix_match_8char_hex():
    22→    """8-char lowercase hex suffix matches a finding ID ending with ::suffix."""
    23→    assert _matches_pattern(_SAMPLE_ID, _SAMPLE_FINDING, "f41b3eb7") is True
    24→
    25→
    26→def test_suffix_no_match_wrong_hex():
    27→    """Different 8-char hex does not match."""
    28→    assert _matches_pattern(_SAMPLE_ID, _SAMPLE_FINDING, "00000000") is False
    29→
    30→
    31→def test_suffix_no_match_too_short():
    32→    """7-char pattern is not treated as a suffix match."""
    33→    assert _matches_pattern(_SAMPLE_ID, _SAMPLE_FINDING, "f41b3eb") is False
    34→
    35→
    36→def test_suffix_no_match_non_hex():
    37→    """Pattern with non-hex chars is not treated as a suffix match."""
    38→    assert _matches_pattern(_SAMPLE_ID, _SAMPLE_FINDING, "f41b3exz") is False
    39→
    40→
    41→def test_suffix_no_match_uppercase():
    42→    """Uppercase hex is not treated as suffix match (finding IDs use lowercase)."""
    43→    assert _matches_pattern(_SAMPLE_ID, _SAMPLE_FINDING, "F41B3EB7") is False
    44→
    45→
    46→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    47→# End-to-end through match_findings
    48→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    49→
    50→
    51→def test_match_findings_via_suffix():
    52→    """Suffix matching works through the match_findings() public API."""
    53→    from desloppify.engine._state.resolution import match_findings
    54→
    55→    state = {
    56→        "findings": {
    57→            _SAMPLE_ID: {
    58→                "id": _SAMPLE_ID,
    59→                "status": "open",
    60→                "detector": "review",
    61→                "file": "test.py",
    62→                "summary": "test",
    63→                "confidence": "medium",
    64→                "tier": 2,
    65→                "detail": {},
    66→            },
    67→        },

   111→def test_name_segment_no_partial_match():

   114→
   115→

   117→    """Pattern containing :: uses prefix rule, not name-segment."""

   119→
   120→
   121→def test_name_segment_does_not_shadow_detector():
   122→    """'review' still matches via detector rule even though it's also a prefix segment."""

  1246→def _find_cluster_for(fid: str, clusters: dict) -> str | None:

  1254→def _count_log_activity_since(plan: dict, since: str) -> dict[str, int]:
  1255→    """Count execution log entries by action since *since* timestamp."""
  1256→    counts: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
  1257→    for entry in plan.get("execution_log", []):
  1258→        if entry.get("timestamp", "") >= since:
  1259→            counts[entry.get("action", "unknown")] += 1
  1260→    return dict(counts)

1→"""Pattern → finding-ID resolution for plan commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel
     6→from desloppify.state import StateModel, match_findings
     7→
     8→
     9→def resolve_ids_from_patterns(
    10→    state: StateModel,
    11→    patterns: list[str],
    12→    *,
    13→    plan: PlanModel | None = None,
    14→    status_filter: str = "open",
    15→) -> list[str]:
    16→    """Resolve one or more patterns to a deduplicated list of finding IDs.
    17→
    18→    When *plan* is provided, literal IDs that exist only in the plan
    19→    (e.g. ``subjective::*`` synthetic items) are included even if they
    20→    have no corresponding entry in ``state["findings"]``.
    21→    """
    22→    seen: set[str] = set()
    23→    result: list[str] = []
    24→
    25→    # Collect all plan IDs for literal-match fallback
    26→    plan_ids: set[str] = set()
    27→    if plan is not None:
    28→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    29→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    30→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    31→            plan_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    32→
    33→    for pattern in patterns:
    34→        matches = match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter)
    35→        if matches:
    36→            for finding in matches:
    37→                fid = finding["id"]
    38→                if fid not in seen:
    39→                    seen.add(fid)
    40→                    result.append(fid)
    41→        elif pattern in plan_ids and pattern not in seen:
    42→            # Literal plan ID (e.g. subjective::foo) not in state findings
    43→            seen.add(pattern)
    44→            result.append(pattern)
    45→    return result
    46→
    47→
    48→__all__ = ["resolve_ids_from_patterns"]
    49→
```

## Implementation Plan: Fix 5 Synthesis Workflow Bugs

### Bug 1 (Critical): `_synthesis_coverage` uses `queue_order` instead of all open review findings

**Root cause**: `_synthesis_coverage` at line 92 of `synthesize_handlers.py` reads `plan.get("queue_order", [])` which only contains the handful of findings explicitly placed in the queue -- not the 46 review findings that exist in state. The function should count ALL open review findings, but it only has access to the plan dict.

**Proposed signature change**:

```python
def _synthesis_coverage(plan: dict, open_review_ids: set[str] | None = None) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
```

When `open_review_ids` is provided, use it as the total set of review IDs. When `None` (backward compatibility, should not happen in practice), fall back to the current `queue_order` logic.

**Implementation**:

Change the function body at lines 98-103 from:
```python
review_ids = [
    fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
    if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
]
```
to:
```python
if open_review_ids is not None:
    review_ids = [fid for fid in open_review_ids if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID]
else:
    review_ids = [
        fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
        if fid != SYNTHESIS_ID and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
    ]
```

**Caller updates** (7 call sites, all in `synthesize_handlers.py`):

| Line | Context | How to get `open_review_ids` |
|------|---------|------------------------------|
| 213 | `_print_progress(plan, open_findings)` | `set(open_findings.keys())` -- `open_findings` is already the review-only dict (passed from `si.open_findings`) |
| 219 | `_apply_completion(args, plan, strategy)` | Lines 231-234 already compute `open_review_ids` from state. Extract that computation to run before line 219, or pass the set computed at lines 231-234 into the call. |
| 795 | `_cmd_synthesize_complete` fold-confirm path | `command_runtime(args).state` is available here (via `_apply_completion` called later). Compute from state: `{fid for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items() if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")}` |
| 831 | `_cmd_synthesize_complete` coverage verification | Same as above -- `state` accessible via `command_runtime(args).state` |
| 885 | `_cmd_synthesize_complete` summary display | Same `command_runtime(args)` |
| 1291 | `_show_plan_summary(plan, state)` | `state` is the second parameter; compute from `state["findings"]` |
| 1526 | `_confirm_organize` | Line 1484-1485 has `state = runtime.state` |

**Helper approach**: Since the computation `{fid for fid, f in state["findings"].items() if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")}` is needed at 5 of the 7 call sites, introduce a small helper:

```python
def _open_review_ids_from_state(state: dict) -> set[str]:
    """Extract the set of open review/concerns finding IDs from state."""
    return {
        fid for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items()
        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
    }
```

Place it near `_synthesis_coverage` (around line 91). Then each call site calls `_synthesis_coverage(plan, _open_review_ids_from_state(state))` or `_synthesis_coverage(plan, set(open_findings.keys()))`.

For `_apply_completion` (line 217-219), refactor to: (1) accept a `state` parameter (it already gets state at line 226), or (2) compute `open_review_ids` first and pass it in. The cleanest approach: since `_apply_completion` already accesses `command_runtime(args).state` at line 226, just move the state access before the coverage call:

```python
def _apply_completion(args: argparse.Namespace, plan: dict, strategy: str) -> None:
    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    state = runtime.state
    open_review = _open_review_ids_from_state(state)
    organized, total, clusters = _synthesis_coverage(plan, open_review)
    ...
```

For `_cmd_synthesize_complete` (lines 795, 831, 885), the function calls `command_runtime(args)` indirectly via `_apply_completion`. To avoid double-calling, compute state once at the top of `_cmd_synthesize_complete` and thread it through. Alternatively, just call `command_runtime(args).state` at each site (it caches internally).

For `_print_progress` (line 213), the `open_findings` dict is the review findings dict from `si.open_findings`. Pass `set(open_findings.keys())` directly.

For `_show_plan_summary` (line 1291), it already receives `state` as a parameter. Compute `_open_review_ids_from_state(state)`.

For `_confirm_organize` (line 1526), `state = runtime.state` is available at line 1485.

---

### Bug 2 (Medium): Name-segment matching misses descriptive name for hashed review IDs

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`, lines 193-199

**Current behavior**: The name-segment fallback matches only the last `::` segment. For IDs like `review::.::holistic::cross_module_architecture::facade_hub_coupling::7fd735cf`, the last segment is the 8-char hex `7fd735cf`. The descriptive name `facade_hub_coupling` is the second-to-last segment.

**Proposed change** at line 193-198:

```python
# Name-segment fallback: bare name matches the last ::segment of the ID
if "::" not in pattern and "::" in finding_id:
    segments = finding_id.split("::")
    name_segment = segments[-1]
    if name_segment == pattern:
        return True
    # For hashed IDs (last segment is 8-char hex), also try
    # the second-to-last segment (the descriptive name)
    if (
        len(segments) >= 3
        and _HEX8_RE.match(name_segment)
        and segments[-2] == pattern
    ):
        return True
```

**Conditions** (as specified in requirements):
- Pattern has no `::` (already checked)
- Finding ID has 3+ segments
- Last segment matches `_HEX8_RE`
- Second-to-last segment exactly equals pattern (exact match, not substring)

This is safe because:
- The `_HEX8_RE` regex is already defined at line 173
- We only add an exact match on the second-to-last segment
- If the last segment is NOT an 8-char hex, the existing behavior is unchanged

---

### Bug 3 (Minor): Cluster overlap warning fires on all existing members

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py`, line 74

**Current code**:
```python
member_set = set(plan["clusters"][cluster_name].get("finding_ids", []))
```

After `add_to_cluster` at line 68, `plan["clusters"][cluster_name]["finding_ids"]` contains ALL members (old + new). The overlap check compares this full set against other clusters, so even findings that were already in the cluster before this add trigger the warning.

**Fix**: Replace `member_set` with `set(finding_ids)` (the IDs that were just requested to be added):

```python
member_set = set(finding_ids)
```

Note: `finding_ids` is the list returned from `resolve_ids_from_patterns` at line 62. These are the IDs the user is trying to add. Some may have already been in the cluster (deduped by `add_to_cluster`), but the overlap warning should only fire when the *newly-requested* IDs overlap significantly with another cluster.

---

### Bug 4 (Minor): Step display double-numbering

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py`, lines 263-265

**Current code**:
```python
for i, step in enumerate(steps, 1):
    print(colorize(f"    {i}. {step}", "dim"))
```

When steps already contain numbering like `"1. Audit..."`, the output shows `1. 1. Audit...`.

**Fix**: Strip leading number+period+space from step text before displaying:

```python
import re  # already imported at top of file? No -- need to add import
```

Actually, checking the imports in `cluster_handlers.py`:

```python
from __future__ import annotations
import argparse
```

No `re` import. Two approaches:

**Option A**: Strip with a regex (requires adding `import re`):
```python
_LEADING_NUMBER_RE = re.compile(r'^\d+\.\s*')

for i, step in enumerate(steps, 1):
    clean = _LEADING_NUMBER_RE.sub('', step)
    print(colorize(f"    {i}. {clean}", "dim"))
```

**Option B**: Simple string processing (no import needed):
```python
for i, step in enumerate(steps, 1):
    clean = step.lstrip()
    # Strip leading "N. " pattern to avoid double-numbering
    if clean and clean[0].isdigit():
        rest = clean.lstrip('0123456789')
        if rest.startswith('. ') or rest.startswith('.'):
            clean = rest.lstrip('. ')
    print(colorize(f"    {i}. {clean}", "dim"))
```

**Recommended**: Option A is cleaner. Add `import re` to the imports at the top of `cluster_handlers.py`.

As noted in the requirements, this is a non-TTY buffering artifact in the worktree subprocess environment, not a product bug.

### Test updates

**1. `test_synthesis_coverage.py`** (existing file):

The existing tests construct plans with `queue_order` and call `_synthesis_coverage(plan)`. These need to be updated:

- Update the test helper `_plan_with_queue` to still build queue_order for backward compat, but the tests should now pass `open_review_ids` explicitly.
- Add a new test `test_coverage_uses_provided_review_ids_not_queue`:

```python
def test_coverage_uses_provided_review_ids_not_queue(self):
    """Coverage total comes from open_review_ids, not queue_order."""
    plan = empty_plan()
    plan["queue_order"] = [SYNTHESIS_ID, "review::test.py::one"]  # only 1 in queue
    plan["clusters"]["c1"] = {
        "finding_ids": ["review::test.py::one", "review::test.py::two"],
        "description": "test",
    }
    # Provide 3 open review IDs -- total should be 3, not 1
    open_ids = {"review::test.py::one", "review::test.py::two", "review::test.py::three"}
    organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan, open_ids)
    assert total == 3
    assert organized == 2  # one and two are in cluster c1
```

- Update existing tests to pass `open_review_ids` parameter (matching the finding IDs they put in `queue_order`, for backward compatibility):

```python
def test_coverage_excludes_non_review_items(self):
    plan = _plan_with_queue(...)
    review_ids = {"review::test.py::naming_issue", "review::test.py::coupling_issue"}
    organized, total, _ = _synthesis_coverage(plan, review_ids)
    assert total == 2
    assert organized == 1
```

**2. `test_suffix_matching.py`** (existing file):

Add tests for second-to-last segment matching:

```python
_HASHED_ID = "review::.::holistic::cross_module_architecture::facade_hub_coupling::7fd735cf"
_HASHED_FINDING = {"detector": "review", "file": "."}

def test_descriptive_name_matches_second_to_last_segment():
    """Bare descriptive name matches second-to-last segment when last is hex hash."""
    assert _matches_pattern(_HASHED_ID, _HASHED_FINDING, "facade_hub_coupling") is True

def test_descriptive_name_no_partial():
    """Partial descriptive name does not match."""
    assert _matches_pattern(_HASHED_ID, _HASHED_FINDING, "facade_hub") is False

def test_second_to_last_only_when_last_is_hex():
    """When last segment is NOT hex, second-to-last is not tried."""
    non_hashed = "review::test.py::naming_issue::some_text"
    finding = {"detector": "review", "file": "test.py"}
    # "naming_issue" is second-to-last but last segment isn't hex
    assert _matches_pattern(non_hashed, finding, "naming_issue") is False
    # "some_text" is last segment, matches normally
    assert _matches_pattern(non_hashed, finding, "some_text") is True

def test_second_to_last_needs_three_segments():
    """IDs with fewer than 3 segments don't try second-to-last match."""
    short_id = "review::abc12345"
    finding = {"detector": "review", "file": "abc12345"}
    # "review" is second-to-last but ID only has 2 segments
    assert _matches_pattern(short_id, finding, "review") is True  # matches via detector
```

**3. `test_cluster_ux.py`** (existing file):

The existing `TestOverlapWarning.test_cluster_add_overlap_warning` test at line 105 should still pass after the fix, because it adds findings `fid1, fid2` to `cluster-b` which are also in `cluster-a`. With the fix, `member_set` becomes `{fid1, fid2}` (the newly added IDs) instead of `plan["clusters"]["cluster-b"]["finding_ids"]` (which is the same after add). So the behavior for this test case is identical.

However, add a new test that demonstrates the bug fix:

```python
def test_cluster_add_no_false_overlap_from_existing_members(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
    """Adding a new finding to a cluster that already has shared members
    does NOT re-warn about pre-existing overlap."""
    fid_shared = "review::test.py::shared"
    fid_new = "review::test.py::new_one"
    state = _state_with_findings(fid_shared, fid_new)
    plan = empty_plan()
    # Both clusters already share fid_shared
    plan["clusters"]["cluster-a"] = {
        "finding_ids": [fid_shared],
        "description": "first",
    }
    plan["clusters"]["cluster-b"] = {
        "finding_ids": [fid_shared],  # already has the shared one
        "description": "second",
    }

    monkeypatch.setattr(cluster_mod, "command_runtime", lambda args: _fake_runtime(state))
    monkeypatch.setattr(cluster_mod, "require_completed_scan", lambda s: True)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cluster_mod, "load_plan", lambda *a, **kw: plan)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cluster_mod, "save_plan", lambda p, *a, **kw: None)

    # Only add fid_new to cluster-b (not the shared one again)
    args = _fake_args(cluster_name="cluster-b", patterns=[fid_new])
    cluster_mod._cmd_cluster_add(args)
    out = capsys.readouterr().out
    # fid_new is not in cluster-a, so no overlap warning
    assert "overlap" not in out.lower()
```

Also add a test for step double-numbering:

```python
def test_cluster_update_no_double_numbering(self, monkeypatch, capsys):
    """Steps with leading numbers don't get double-numbered in display."""
    plan = empty_plan()
    plan["clusters"]["my-cluster"] = {"finding_ids": ["f1"], "description": "test"}
    monkeypatch.setattr(cluster_mod, "load_plan", lambda *a, **kw: plan)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cluster_mod, "save_plan", lambda p, *a, **kw: None)
    args = _fake_args(cluster_name="my-cluster", steps=["1. Audit code", "2. Fix imports"])
    cluster_mod._cmd_cluster_update(args)
    out = capsys.readouterr().out
    assert "1. 1." not in out  # no double numbering
    assert "1. Audit code" in out
    assert "2. Fix imports" in out
```
